/rss[1]/channel[1]/image[1]/url[1]=https://artwork.captivate.fm/dcc31828-5641-48b2-ad5e-cdbb28a56837/2puhpf4gni9tc4fpqbyg0jzi0xhv.jpg
/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[10]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Mike was boarding a plane to head back from a family event this past summer. When he plopped down, little did he know that he was sitting next to an accomplished doctor with more letters after her name than you can shake a stick at.</p><p><strong>Dr. Adrienne Adler-Neal MD PhD MPH</strong> isn't your typical psychiatrist. She studied the neuroscience of mindfulness meditation now runs a private practice that treats the whole person. Psychological. Biological. Spiritual. She says medications can help, but they are never the full answer.</p><p>In this episode she explains why so many people keep looking outside themselves for the fix and why that approach keeps them stuck. She walks through how she helps patients build an internal place they can return to when life gets hard. She shares the exact practice she uses before hard days. She talks about becoming a mother while holding other people’s pain. And she gives the simple daily steps that actually move people toward a life that feels like theirs.</p><p>If you've been vibrating at the wrong frequency or needed a nudge to spend a few minutes in meditation, this episode is for you.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>How a chance plane ticket turned into this conversation</li><li>Why 17-plus years of schooling forced her to find meaning inside herself</li><li>The difference between treating symptoms and creating a life of real alignment</li><li>What most people get wrong about psychiatric medication</li><li>The heart-centered practice that works when traditional meditation feels impossible</li><li>How she protects herself from absorbing patient pain after becoming a parent</li><li>Telehealth versus in-person and how she decides</li><li>Addiction, the 12 steps, and why peer support matters</li><li>The advice she gives clients that she still struggles to take herself</li><li>Three practical things anyone can start doing today</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters </strong></p><ul><li>00:00 Crisis resources and intro</li><li>02:08 Welcome and how Mike met Dr. Adler-Neal on a plane</li><li>03:56 What’s your story? Ballet dancer or doctor?</li><li>05:30 Building an internal locus of meaning during the PhD years</li><li>07:06 17 and a half years of advanced schooling</li><li>08:35 Why psychiatry and the gift of having a place to return to</li><li>09:49 Growth potential instead of “everyone is broken”</li><li>11:34 Blending Eastern and Western approaches without fighting</li><li>12:28 Heart-centered compassion when the mind won’t quiet</li><li>14:14 Isolation, connection, and what people struggle with most</li><li>15:20 “Invite it in for tea”</li><li>16:28 Holding patient pain without carrying it home</li><li>18:20 The mentor and the book that redirected her entire path</li><li>20:20 Motherhood and switching from doctor to mom</li><li>21:40 Telehealth effectiveness and when in-person is needed</li><li>23:24 Addiction work and the power of the 12 steps</li><li>24:29 The advice she gives that she doesn’t always take</li><li>25:00 What pop culture gets wrong about psychiatric medication</li><li>26:32 Medications are not a quick fix and nothing external is the answer</li><li>27:14 Stabilization first. Getting people to zero so real work can begin</li><li>29:29 Three everyday practices for staying grounded</li><li>30:50 Purpose, social media comparison, and protecting kids’ brains</li><li>33:40 Adrienneisms and “invite it in for tea”</li><li>34:20 Herbalism potions and learning to play bad golf</li><li>35:28 Closing and thank you</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Episode Links</strong></p><ul><li><strong>Instagram:</strong> <a href="https://instagram.com/@dradlerneal" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">https://instagram.com/@dradlerneal</a></li><li><strong>Dr. Adler-Neal's website:</strong> <a href="https://www.adlerneal.com/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">https://www.adlerneal.com</a></li><li><strong>US Crisis Hotline:</strong> <a href="https://988lifeline.org" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">https://988lifeline.org</a></li></ul><br/><p><strong>If you or someone you know needs support, call or text the 988 Suicide &amp; /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[10]/title[1]=Why You Feel Stuck (And How to Fix It) | Dr. Adrienne Adler-Neal
/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[11]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Former Maricopa County Sheriff Paul Penzone lost once to Sheriff Joe Arpaio after getting outspent 13 to 1. His wife heard him complaining at the TV, took his man card, and told him to step up again.</p><p>He did. Then he won by 13 points.</p><p>Paul tells Mike Lindstrom and Scott Leese the part most people never hear. He was a volunteer bailiff in high school. He spent 21 years at Phoenix PD. He ran as a Democrat after Republican strategists told him he had no shot. He refused to endorse anybody, including two presidential candidates, because he did not want the badge used as a political weapon.</p><p>His first week on the job, a naked woman stole a patrol car in Casa Grande. A deputy grabbed a blanket to cover her. She jumped in the car and took off. That is the job.</p><p>Paul also gets into the hard stuff. Culture over entertainment. Why he closed the circus around Tent City and pink jumpsuits. Why deadly force is not a choice, it is the lack of a choice. Why cameras and tools can steal the skill of talking someone into handcuffs. And what it costs a family when you have to knock on a door and tell a wife she is a widow.</p><p>If you have ever been told you cannot win, this one will sit with you.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>Paul’s path from Trenton, New Jersey to Cortez High School and Phoenix PD</li><li>The courtroom summers that taught him to seek the truth and speak the truth</li><li>Why he will not let a bully have the last word</li><li>The living room fight with his wife that launched the race against Joe Arpaio</li><li>Losing in 2012 by four points, then getting a birthday job rejection that sent him back</li><li>Election night 2016 with no TVs, no radios, and 35 people in a sandwich shop</li><li>Joe Arpaio’s concession call after 24 years in office</li><li>The first change he made as sheriff: culture</li><li>Why law enforcement should work like a good referee, not a reality show</li><li>Cameras, Big Brother, and what happens when discretion disappears</li><li>How body cams and 2020 changed an officer’s instincts on a stop</li><li>Tasers versus talking, and why he never once maced anyone</li><li>“The use of deadly force is not a choice. It is the lack of a choice.”</li><li>Leaving the badge without letting the title eat your identity</li><li>Praying the rosary, Sundays with no detail, and four line-of-duty death notifications</li><li>The bathroom stall at D-backs stadium and what it costs to be “on” everywhere</li><li>The mantra: do not live for the approval of people</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 First week as sheriff, a naked lady steals a patrol car</li><li>01:27 Welcome to What’s Your Story with Mike and Scott</li><li>02:25 Paul’s story, from Trenton to Phoenix PD</li><li>04:22 Volunteer bailiff and seeking the truth</li><li>06:31 Why he never backed down from bullies</li><li>08:38 Taking on Sheriff Joe Arpaio</li><li>13:08 Losing in 2012, then running again</li><li>16:12 Election night 2016 with no TVs</li><li>18:42 Joe Arpaio’s concession call</li><li>19:40 Culture over entertainment</li><li>22:46 Cameras, Big Brother, and discretion</li><li>24:42 How body cams changed everyday stops</li><li>28:49 Deadly force is the lack of a choice</li><li>29:22 Leaving the badge without losing yourself</li><li>31:48 Family, faith, and knocking on a widow’s door</li><li>35:15 The bathroom stall and being “on” as sheriff</li><li>37:17 The mantra he lives by</li><li>38:32 Outro</li></ul><br/><p>If this one hit, subscribe and send it to somebody who still thinks one person cannot move a machine that big. Then tell us in the comments. When did somebody tell you that you had no shot, and what did you do with that?</p><p><strong>Thanks for watching!</strong> If this episode moved you, hit LIKE, drop a comment with your biggest takeaway, and <strong>SUBSCRIBE</strong> for more raw stories, mindset shifts, and real conversations.</p><p>New episodes drop every Tuesday morning!</p><ul><li><strong>TikTok:</strong> <u><a href="https://tiktok.com/@whatsyourstorypod_com" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">tiktok.com/@whatsyourstorypod_com</a></u></li><li><strong>Instagram:</strong> <u><a href="https://instagram.com/whatsyourstorypod_com" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">instagram.com/whatsyourstorypod_com</a></u></li><li><strong>X:</strong> <u><a href="https://x.com/WYSPodcast_com" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">x.com/WYSPodcast_com</a></u></li><li><strong>Facebook:</strong> <u><a href="https://facebook.com/WhatsYourStoryPod" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">facebook.com/WhatsYourStoryPod</a></u></li><li><strong>LinkedIn:</strong> <a href="https://www.linkedin.com/company/whatsyourstorypod" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">linkedin.com/company/whatsyourstorypod</a></li></ul><br/><p><strong>This episode is sponsored by <a href="https://e3wealth.com" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">e3 wealth</a> &amp; <a href="https://nectarflow.com" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">NectarFlow</a>.</strong> They are absolutely amazing, so please support them by checking them out today and tell them Mike &amp; Scott sent you.</p><p><strong>Websites</strong></p><ul><li><strong>New Show Website:</strong> <a href="https://whatsyourstorypod.com" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">whatsyourstorypod.com</a></li><li><strong>Mike's Website:</strong> <u><a href="https://mikelindstrom.com/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">mikelindstrom.com</a></u></li><li><strong>Scott's Website:</strong> <u><a href="https://scottleeseconsulting.com/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">scottleeseconsulting.com</a></u></li></ul><br/>

/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[12]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Susan McGalla walked away from the presidency of American Eagle Outfitters at its peak. She helped grow the brand from $300 million to $3 billion. The company was her baby. Those people were her family. Leaving to raise her kids was gut-wrenching.</p><p>She never looked back. That choice opened every door after. Including the one her son helped open at Brophy Prep. She now serves as Vice President for Advancement and Strategic Communications. Her office sits on the same campus where her son goes to school. He stops by for snacks. Friends leave sticky notes on her computer. She is exactly where she wants to be.</p><p>In this episode of What’s Your Story? with Mike &amp; Scott, Susan shares the full arc. Ohio sports family roots. Early retail days that left her sure she would get fired. The leap to American Eagle in her late twenties. The three principles behind the explosive growth. Hard truths about career and family choices women still face. Building Steelers Nation Unite. And the Brophy classroom moment that pulled her out of retirement.</p><p>She talks about mentors who saw more in her than she saw in herself. The family motto she repeated every morning dropping the kids at school. And why the most meaningful success is still the life you touch.</p><p>This one is full of heart, hard-earned wisdom, and full-circle moments that stick.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered:</strong></p><ul><li>The gut-wrenching decision to leave American Eagle Outfitters at the height of its success</li><li>Growing a single brand from $300 million to $3 billion while staying profitable</li><li>The three principles behind that growth: daring to dream, fiscal responsibility, and staying small as you scale</li><li>Hard choices women still face between career and family</li><li>How her son brought her out of retirement and into the Brophy Prep role</li><li>What “advancement” really means at a high school and why the work matters</li><li>Building the Steelers Nation Unite loyalty program and combining sports with business</li><li>Mentors who believed in her before she fully believed in herself</li><li>Family values, a daily motto, and the quiet power of /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[12]/title[1]=She Left a Fortune to Raise Her Kids — Now She's Back | Susan McGalla
/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[13]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Dan Lier walked into the Mayo Clinic thinking he might die that night. Doctors told him he had cardiac amyloidosis — a rare blood disease that kills 40% of patients in the first 12 months and only 10% make it past 10 years. He’s in year 9.</p><p>In this powerful episode of What’s Your Story? with Mike &amp; Scott, Dan goes deep on the mindset that refused to accept the statistics. From growing up on welfare and food stamps, to winning two national basketball championships as the 9th or 10th most athletic player on the roster, to becoming Tony Robbins’ top field leader after an integrity moment that could have ended his career… this is the unfiltered story of a 6’8” mindset architect who turned every setback into fuel.</p><p>Dan shares the moments of real fear, the nightly affirmation audio he created while going through two years of chemo, how he became one of the top amyloidosis influencers in the country (and worked with Pfizer), and why he still believes every obstacle is preparing you for something bigger.</p><p>If you’ve ever faced a diagnosis, a career crossroads, or a moment that felt like the end — this conversation will reframe everything.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>Growing up on public assistance and the mindset shift that started everything</li><li>Two national championships at Fort Hays State and the psychology of hustle over talent</li><li>The wacky coach who hung upside down like a bat and rewired Dan’s brain</li><li>Selling insurance, reading Tony Robbins, and becoming the #1 producer at 23</li><li>The integrity moment that got him promoted to Tony Robbins’ leadership team</li><li>Leaving Robbins, starting his own seminars in LA, and building a keynote empire in Vegas</li><li>The cardiac amyloidosis diagnosis, Mayo Clinic, chemo, and the stats that should have broken him</li><li>Becoming an amyloidosis influencer and advocate almost by accident</li><li>How the disease made him more patient, present, and selective with his time</li><li>The power of nightly affirmations and programming the mind for healing</li><li>Why 90% of success is psychology — and how to actually change behavior</li><li>Current work with Janek Performance Group and ongoing rare disease advocacy</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 – Cold Open: "40% Die in Year One — I'll Take the Other 60%"</li><li>01:38 – Welcome: Mike's Most Personal Episode Yet</li><li>02:04 – Meet Dan Lier — Champion, Tony Robbins Leader, Rare Disease Survivor</li><li>02:31 – How Dan Single-Handedly Destroyed Mike's Legal Career</li><li>02:48 – Small-Town Michigan to Two-Time National Champion</li><li>04:11 – From Basketball to Insurance: The Accidental Salesman</li><li>04:33 – Welfare, Food Stamps &amp; a Dad Who Hated Salespeople</li><li>06:28 – Finding Tony Robbins at 23 — Tripled His Income, Became #1</li><li>06:46 – The Championship Secret: Hustle Over Talent</li><li>08:43 – The Coach Who Hung Upside Down Like a Bat</li><li>11:00 – "I'm a Mindset Architect, Not a Motivational Speaker"</li><li>13:57 – The $400 Theft That Almost Ended Everything… Then Got Him Promoted</li><li>16:19 – The Baby Monitor Betrayal — Hearing a Friend Trash Him</li><li>19:50 – Turning Down Tony Robbins &amp; Starting Over in Hermosa Beach</li><li>21:00 – "I Think I'm Gonna Die Tonight" — The Red-Eye Flight to Mayo Clinic</li><li>24:42 – The Diagnosis: 40% Die in 12 Months, Only 10% Survive 10 Years</li><li>26:15 – How Chemo Videos Accidentally Made Him an Amyloidosis Influencer</li><li>28:00 – Why a Near-Death Diagnosis Made Him a Better Man</li><li>31:16 – The Nightly Affirmation Audio That Kept Him Alive</li><li>35:40 – What's Next: Keynotes, Janek &amp; Rare Disease Advocacy</li><li>37:17 – Dan's Closing Lesson: Everything Happens FOR You</li><li>38:41 – "I'm 63 and Just Opening the Biggest Chapter of My Life"</li><li>40:27 – Sign-Off &amp; Outro</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Connect with Dan Lier</strong></p><ul><li>Website: https://danlier.com</li><li>Instagram: https://instagram.com/danlier</li><li>X: /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[13]/description[1]=<p>Dan Lier walked into the Mayo Clinic thinking he might die that night. Doctors told him he had cardiac amyloidosis — a rare blood disease that kills 40% of patients in the first 12 months and only 10% make it past 10 years. He’s in year 9.</p><p>In this powerful episode of What’s Your Story? with Mike &amp; Scott, Dan goes deep on the mindset that refused to accept the statistics. From growing up on welfare and food stamps, to winning two national basketball championships as the 9th or 10th most athletic player on the roster, to becoming Tony Robbins’ top field leader after an integrity moment that could have ended his career… this is the unfiltered story of a 6’8” mindset architect who turned every setback into fuel.</p><p>Dan shares the moments of real fear, the nightly affirmation audio he created while going through two years of chemo, how he became one of the top amyloidosis influencers in the country (and worked with Pfizer), and why he still believes every obstacle is preparing you for something bigger.</p><p>If you’ve ever faced a diagnosis, a career crossroads, or a moment that felt like the end — this conversation will reframe everything.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>Growing up on public assistance and the mindset shift that started everything</li><li>Two national championships at Fort Hays State and the psychology of hustle over talent</li><li>The wacky coach who hung upside down like a bat and rewired Dan’s brain</li><li>Selling insurance, reading Tony Robbins, and becoming the #1 producer at 23</li><li>The integrity moment that got him promoted to Tony Robbins’ leadership team</li><li>Leaving Robbins, starting his own seminars in LA, and building a keynote empire in Vegas</li><li>The /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[14]/title[1]=This "Swiftie Liftie" Reps in the Gym AND on the Roster! | Alexy Posner
/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[15]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Today we have not one, but two special guests! You're in for a treat.</p><p>What happens when families in crisis hand their kids over to a loosely regulated $23 billion industry promising to "fix" them? <strong>Professor Jon McQueen</strong>, a survivor of the Troubled Teen Industry (TTI), pulls back the curtain on wilderness programs, residential boarding schools, and the devastating human cost—450+ deaths and 220,000 kids sent yearly, often starting with a middle-of-the-night "transport."</p><p>Jon shares his raw journey from Chicago kid to sales leader (realtor.com and more) to full-time ASU sociology professor teaching 1,900 students. He breaks down AI cheating dilemmas in education, why students feel unseen, and his groundbreaking <em>Men &amp; Masculinity</em> class that challenges toxic narratives with multiple masculinities.</p><p>Later, NFL legend and returning guest <strong>Nick Lowery</strong> joins for the final stretch—talking his new book <em>Naked and Alone with 80,000 People</em> (now on Audible!), leadership lessons from the Chiefs, factory keynotes, mental health, and his free Companion app helping first responders and beyond.</p><p>This episode is packed with real talk on resilience, education's evolution, fatherhood, team chemistry, and creating connection in a disconnected world.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>Troubled Teen Industry (TTI) survivor story &amp; exposé</li><li>Sales-to-academia career pivot and "hacking" the system</li><li>Teaching massive classes and making students feel seen</li><li>AI cheating crisis, detectors, and mental atrophy in education</li><li>Multiple masculinities vs toxic masculinity debates</li><li>Public speaking fears and using sales skills in academia</li><li>Worst-to-first leadership and team chemistry (Chiefs stories)</li><li>Mental health companion app for first responders &amp; veterans</li><li>Fan connection, social capital, and performing under pressure</li><li>RateMyProfessor feedback and evolving with student needs</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>0:00 Cold Open: The Troubled Teen Industry</li><li>0:40 Intro Band &amp; McKenna shares her journey from Huntington Beach to the University of San Diego, where she played alongside her identical twin sister Mikayla. She opens up about training with future national team players, the unique bond and challenges of competing as twins, and how comparison almost derailed their relationship.</p><p>The conversation explores what it was really like growing up in a sports-obsessed family, getting recruited into club soccer, and navigating the identity crisis that hit after college athletics ended. McKenna gets honest about a painful breakup, moving home to Huntington Beach for a season of self-work and renewed faith, and the leap of faith that led her to Scottsdale where she finally figured out who she truly is.</p><p>She also shares her current career reflections after years of remote work and her growing excitement about transitioning into residential real estate, where she can help people with one of life's biggest decisions while staying true to her natural self. The episode closes with a fun rapid-fire round that reveals her favorite movie, current book, biggest influence, and the mom-ism she will never forget.</p><p>Whether you are a former athlete searching /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[17]/content:encoded[1]=<p>In this raw and powerful episode of What's Your Story? with Mike Lindstrom &amp; Scott Leese, we sit down with Chris Powell — the fitness transformation expert and former host of ABC’s Extreme Weight Loss. Chris opens up about the darkest chapter of his life: herniating a disc, getting hooked on painkillers, losing everything, and living out of his car.</p><p>He reveals the powerful epiphany that led him to tattoo “1 through a million” down his side and commit to changing that many lives. Chris also shares the incredible true story of walking into a stranger’s home to help a 630-pound man named David lose 400 pounds — a friendship that went viral on MySpace, landed them on Oprah and 20/20, and ultimately helped Chris land his own national TV show.</p><p>But the journey wasn’t one-sided. When Chris hit rock bottom, the man he saved was there to save him right back. Chris also breaks down why “hope” is the real missing ingredient in weight loss, how “Power Promises” build unstoppable momentum, his honest take on GLP-1 medications (Ozempic, Wegovy, etc.), and why the body positivity movement can sometimes do more harm than good.</p><p>If you’ve ever struggled with weight, battled addiction, lost hope, or are searching for sustainable transformation, this conversation will hit hard and give you practical tools you can use today.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered:</strong></p><ul><li>Chris Powell’s early TV breakthrough and bold networking story</li><li>The unforgettable story of helping David lose 400 pounds</li><li>Chris’s personal battle with painkiller addiction and rock bottom</li><li>The tattoo mission that saved his life (1 through a million)</li><li>How David saved Chris right back</li><li>Landing the ABC Extreme Weight Loss show deal</li><li>David’s later struggles and heartbreaking passing</li><li>The #1 misconception holding people back in health &amp; fitness</li><li>Why hope beats quick fixes + the “Power Promise” method</li><li>Transitioning from network TV to social media success</li><li>Handling haters and the body positivity debate</li><li>Chris’s new role as Chief Transformation Officer at Thrive Lab</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters:</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 - Podcast Intro &amp; Living in His Car</li><li>08:04 - The Epiphany &amp; The “1 Through a Million” Tattoo</li><li>08:35 - How the Man He Saved Saved Him Back</li><li>09:50 - Viral on MySpace, Oprah, 20/20 &amp; Japanese Game Show</li><li>10:40 - “You’re the Host!” — The Shocking ABC Phone Call</li><li>12:27 - David’s Rollercoaster Life &amp; Heartbreaking Passing</li><li>13:49 - The Biggest Misconception in Health &amp; Fitness Today</li><li>16:34 - “Hope” Is the Real Game Changer + Power Promises</li><li>18:25 - From Traditional TV Star to Social Media Reality</li><li>20:30 - Multi-Platform Content Strategy That Actually Works</li><li>24:30 - Handling Haters &amp; /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[18]/content:encoded[1]=<p>What happens when a former walk-on quarterback who built and sold multiple private-equity-backed companies gets fed up with Arizona’s broken political system and decides to run for governor as a true independent?</p><p>Hugh Lytle isn’t your typical politician. He’s a healthcare rebel who transformed entire segments of the industry by fixing what was broken. Now he’s bringing that same “get it done” mindset to Arizona politics — and he’s not asking for permission from either party.</p><p>In this episode, Hugh opens up about the brutal legal battles he’s already fought just to stay on the ballot, the realistic math for an independent to actually win in Arizona, and why he believes this is the moment for a real third voice. He also reveals Hugh AI — a digital twin of himself that voters can talk to 24/7.</p><p>This is raw, honest, and very different from the usual political interview.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered:</strong></p><ul><li>The “walk-on mentality” that shaped his entire career and approach to business</li><li>How he built and scaled major healthcare companies through private equity</li><li>Why he shifted from center-right to center-left after seeing healthcare disparities up close</li><li>The insane legal obstacles independents face in Arizona (and how he’s beating them)</li><li>The realistic path and math for an independent to win the governorship</li><li>His vision to turn Arizona into a global knowledge economy leader</li><li>Launching Hugh AI — a 24/7 digital version of himself for direct voter access</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters:</strong></p><p>00:00 – Cold open: Hugh’s frustration with two-tier healthcare</p><p>01:50 – Welcome Hugh Lytle (our first politician on the show)</p><p>03:05 – Is he still the same guy he was growing up?</p><p>04:04 – Midwest values, walk-on QB at ASU, and the rebel mindset</p><p>07:30 – Why he became a “healthcare rebel” and built massive companies</p><p>13:00 – The moment he realized healthcare was broken for entire communities</p><p>17:00 – How building companies prepared him for politics</p><p>20:00 – Why he’s running as an independent + the brutal legal fights</p><p>27:00 – The math for an independent to actually win in Arizona</p><p>33:00 – Hugh AI: His 24/7 digital twin for voters</p><p>35:00 – Final thoughts + where to follow the campaign</p><p>What’s one thing you wish government would actually fix instead of just arguing about? /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[1]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Kim Alexis replaced Lauren Hutton at Revlon, then walked away from modeling at 27.</p><p>She told Mike Lindstrom and Scott Leese how a Lockport tomboy who wanted to be a pharmacist ended up on magazine covers within months of turning 18.</p><p>She still shows up. She still pulls stories out of people. She still calls herself the dash between born and dead.</p><p>Kim Alexis (@the_kimalexis) launched Unexpired after raising three sons and realizing her real passion was the interview itself. The podcast name came from relevance. She is still here. She still has something to say.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>How a modeling school discovery in Buffalo flipped a pharmacist plan</li><li>The speed from high school graduation to Rome covers and a Glamour contract</li><li>Replacing Lauren Hutton and learning the agency relationship was not built for her</li><li>Jumping live on Good Morning America the morning after she was hired</li><li>Why Unexpired started as a health show and became a story show</li><li>Self-advocacy lessons from the 70s and 80s modeling world</li><li>Studio 54, crying the first two years, and finding peace alone in the apartment</li><li>How sports discipline helped her say no when the industry did not</li><li>Casting calls that now ask for follower counts instead of tear sheets</li><li>What changed when actresses took the makeup and skincare ads</li><li>Common threads she sees across guests: preparation, showing up, saying yes</li><li>Handling critics with one word and compartmentalizing the rest</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 Cold Open: Beans and Weenies</li><li>00:57 Show Intro</li><li>01:52 Hosts Welcome Kim Alexis</li><li>02:40 Scott</strong>, Dave reveals how he removed layers of emotional “armor,” reclaimed his authentic self, and now helps athletes, entrepreneurs, and high-performers raise their frequency, drop limiting beliefs, and create extraordinary results.</p><p>He shares the exact daily tuning routine that led to an instant house-sale manifestation and breaks down the difference between energy-poor and energy-super-rich states.</p><p>If you’ve ever felt stuck, broken, or like you lost your identity after a major life event, this conversation will give you hope and practical tools to rise.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered:</strong></p><ul><li>Near-death experience and spiritual awakening after NHL concussion</li><li>Brain injury recovery, micro-hemorrhaging, and identity loss after sports career</li><li>Removing emotional armor, trauma imprints, and becoming your authentic self</li><li>Raising personal frequency/vibration for healing, success, and manifestation</li><li>Vulnerability as a superpower in coaching and leadership</li><li>Energy states (poor → neutral → rich → super rich) and how to tune in daily</li><li>Practical routines: meditation, visualization, gratitude, movement, and hydration</li><li>Lessons from NHL fights, hat tricks, and flow-state performance</li><li>Coaching pro athletes and scaling to help more people (post-COVID shift)</li><li>Servant leadership and co-creation with a higher power</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters:</strong></p><p>00:00 — Dave Opens with His Fifth Concussion &amp; /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[21]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Truitt Robinson joins the show as our youngest guest ever — an ASU Cronkite grad, Phoenix Suns video intern, and video editor who’s lived a very different college experience than most.</p><p>He opens up about moving from a small Alabama town to Ohio, then surviving rough living situations in Phoenix while grinding as a videographer. Truitt shares how ADHD and Asperger’s shaped his early years, the bullying he faced, and the powerful shift that happened when he got serious about fitness, Brazilian jiu-jitsu, and self-improvement.</p><p>He also gives a candid look at the Gen Z job market in 2026 — ghosting, AI disruption, insane competition, and why even a perfect resume often isn’t enough. Truitt talks about the pressure of “the grass is always greener,” why pace of the leader still matters, and his advice for young people trying to earn real respect.</p><p><strong>Chapters:</strong></p><p>00:00 – Get in Shape First: Truitt’s #1 Advice for Young People</p><p>01:32 – Podcast Intro &amp; Welcome to Truitt Robinson</p><p>02:19 – Truitt’s Story: Alabama Roots, Ohio Move &amp; Finding His Way to ASU</p><p>04:01 – Tough College Years Living in Phoenix (Rough Areas &amp; Mental Toll)</p><p>07:00 – The Turning Point: Moving to Tempe &amp; Finally Feeling Like a College Kid</p><p>09:03 – The Scary Reality of the Gen Z Job Market in 2026</p><p>13:23 – ADHD, Asperger’s, Bullying &amp; How Getting in Shape Changed Everything</p><p>16:30 – Video Editing Journey, Student Emmy &amp; Landing the Phoenix Suns Internship</p><p>20:08 – Pace of the Leader: Why Truitt Turns Down Work When Leaders Won’t Show Up</p><p>22:00 – Rapid Fire, Philadelphia Eagles Fandom &amp; Final Advice</p><p><strong>Links:</strong></p><ul><li>Truitt on Instagram / TikTok / YouTube / X: @TRobVidz</li></ul><br/><p>What’s one piece of advice you’d give to someone graduating right now? /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[23]/content:encoded[1]=<p>What if AI isn’t about replacing tasks… but about becoming something <em>greater</em>?</p><p>Patrick Antrim (NectarFlow CEO and our studio “landlord”) returns to the show and shares the exact mindset shift every leader needs right now: Stop asking “What can AI do for me?” and start asking “What can I become with AI?”</p><p>In this episode he breaks down:</p><ul><li>Why most companies are using AI wrong (and creating noise instead of value)</li><li>NectarFlow’s Picasso Hire – decision intelligence that finds the right people before the interview</li><li>RAMP – ensuring new hires actually understand what to do in their first 60-90 days</li><li>How to design organizations for the future instead of managing what you already have</li><li>Multifamily and insurance as the beachhead (and why it applies to every industry)</li></ul><br/><p>If you’re hiring, leading, or scaling in the AI era, this conversation will change how you think about talent, execution, and growth.</p><p><strong>Chapters:</strong></p><p>00:00 – Studio “landlord” returns</p><p>01:25 – Podcast intro</p><p>02:20 – What NectarFlow actually does</p><p>06:00 – The big AI mindset shift: “What can I become?”</p><p>09:00 – Picasso Hire: Decision intelligence for talent selection</p><p>12:30 – RAMP: Onboarding that actually works</p><p>16:00 – Why hiring is no longer an HR conversation</p><p>20:00 – Execution advantage in the AI era</p><p>24:00 – Multifamily &amp; /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[24]/content:encoded[1]=<p>What if the book that changed millions of lives started as nothing more than a brochure to sell a board game?</p><p>Sharon Lechter — co-author of <em>Rich Dad Poor Dad</em>, 29 books in 29 years, five-time New York Times bestseller, and lifelong financial literacy crusader — sits down for a masterclass on money, mindset, and legacy.</p><p>In this powerful conversation, Sharon shares:</p><ul><li>The hilarious (and slightly “kinky”) story of how her husband introduced her to Robert Kiyosaki</li><li>How <em>Rich Dad Poor Dad</em> was written in weeks as a simple brochure — and then took on a life of its own</li><li>Why her son’s credit card debt in 1992 became the spark for her life’s mission</li><li>The Personal Success Equation that turns fear into faith (Passion + Talent × Association × Action + Faith)</li><li>Why “Assets are sexy” — and how to build true wealth beyond dollars</li><li>Her new book <em>Old Wealth, New Wealth, True Wealth</em> with 26-year-old real estate millionaire Nathan Barkocy</li><li>When to close a door (the Tums &amp; Rolaids test) and the power of association</li></ul><br/><p>If you’ve ever wondered how to shift from scarcity to abundance, build real assets, or leave a legacy that actually matters — this episode is required listening.</p><p><strong>Chapters:</strong></p><p>00:00 – Sharon’s hilarious “kinky” husband story that started it all</p><p>01:21 – Podcast intro with Mike &amp; Scott</p><p>02:00 – Lower middle-class roots, accounting days &amp; first entrepreneurship</p><p>03:36 – Son’s credit card debt sparks her life’s mission in financial literacy</p><p>06:03 – The full origin story of meeting Robert Kiyosaki</p><p>08:18 – How <em>Rich Dad Poor Dad</em> was written as a brochure for a $200 board game</p><p>12:04 – Writing 29 books: why every book is a different journey</p><p>14:10 – The incredible story behind <em>Outwitting the Devil</em></p><p>19:00 – New book <em>Old Wealth, New Wealth, True Wealth</em> with Nathan Barcachi</p><p>23:25 – The power of storytelling that made <em>Rich Dad</em> explode</p><p>28:00 – Personal Success Equation: Passion + Talent × Association × Action + Faith</p><p>33:00 – The Tums &amp; Rolaids test: when to close a door and walk away</p><p>37:00 – Speaking, mentoring, Cherry Creek Lodge retreats &amp; what’s next</p><p>39:00 – Final advice &amp; where to find Sharon</p><p><strong>Drop your biggest takeaway in the comments</strong> — was it the origin story, the Personal Success Equation, or the “Assets are sexy” mindset? 👇</p><p>Subscribe for more unfiltered conversations with legends who’ve built real wealth and real impact.</p><p><strong>Links:</strong></p><ul><li>Sharon’s website: <a href="SharonLechter.com" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">SharonLechter.com</a></li><li>New book <em>Old Wealth, New Wealth, True Wealth</em> on <a href="https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0GHLC44HD?ref=cm_sw_r_ffobk_cp_ud_dp_GTF9EB1KJQG9JH0P6X8M&amp;ref_=cm_sw_r_ffobk_cp_ud_dp_GTF9EB1KJQG9JH0P6X8M&amp;social_share=cm_sw_r_ffobk_cp_ud_dp_GTF9EB1KJQG9JH0P6X8M&amp;bestFormat=true" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Amazon</a></li><li>Cherry Creek Lodge retreats: <a href="CherryCreekLodge.com" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">CherryCreekLodge.com</a></li></ul><br/>

/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[25]/content:encoded[1]=<p>What if the success you’ve been chasing for 25 years suddenly felt empty once your kids became adults?</p><p>John Moriarty, founder of e3 Wealth and co-author of <em>The Entrepreneur Playbook</em>, opens up about the moment everything changed. After decades of grinding as a financial planner and entrepreneur, he realized “enoughness” wasn’t another zero on the balance sheet — it was protecting the family he had unintentionally left behind while building the business.</p><p>In this raw and powerful conversation, John opens up about:</p><ul><li>Growing up lower middle class and the moment he decided “that will never be my family’s story”</li><li>The painful conversation with his wife that changed everything</li><li>Why he now teaches “enoughness” instead of endless growth</li><li>How an introvert runs a successful wealth firm without burning out</li><li>His surprisingly smart (and compliant-approved) way to enjoy Vegas, wine, and golf</li><li>The one money conversation every parent is avoiding… but shouldn’t</li></ul><br/><p>If you’re an entrepreneur, parent, or high-earner who feels like you’re “winning” but still feels something’s missing — this conversation will hit different.</p><p><strong>Chapters:</strong></p><p>00:00 – The wild Mayfair Supper Club roller-skate show in Vegas</p><p>02:43 – John’s raw elevator pitch (and why he’s an introvert who loves people)</p><p>09:35 – What “Enoughness” really means to a 25-year financial planner</p><p>10:00 – The beach conversation with his wife that broke him (and healed their family)</p><p>13:59 – How he mentors (and why most people shouldn’t try to be like him)</p><p>16:07 – His son’s internship reality check: “90% of work is boring… and that’s the point”</p><p>28:57 – The childhood money moment that still drives him today</p><p>33:14 – How he &amp; Born to a single mom, haunted by an absent father who was in and out of prison, Luke spiraled into rage, multiple DUIs, bar fights, and self-destruction — until a near-miss with his newborn daughter became the wake-up call that changed everything.</p><p>Now 17+ years sober, Luke opens up about intentional suffering, plant medicine journeys, surrendering to faith, and building <em>The Crucible</em> — his legendary 32-hour men’s transformation experience that combines physical, mental, emotional, and spiritual challenges.</p><p>If you’re a man struggling with purpose, identity, trauma, or just ready for the next level — this episode hits hard.</p><p><strong>Key Topics:</strong></p><ul><li>Growing up fatherless and the rage that followed</li><li>Rock bottom: DUIs, fights, losing everything</li><li>The moment his daughter was born that forced change</li><li>Sobriety, CrossFit, and rebuilding identity</li><li>Plant medicine, breathwork &amp; intentional healing</li><li>The Crucible: 32-hour men’s transformation (what really happens)</li><li>Faith, surrender, and becoming the man you needed</li><li>Fathers of the Future movement &amp; helping the next generation</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters:</strong></p><p>00:00 Intro &amp; Luke Kayyem’s Powerful Story Begins</p><p>01:53 Fatherless Childhood &amp; Early Trauma</p><p>04:45 From Rage &amp; Alcohol to Sobriety Wake-Up Call</p><p>08:20 Building The Crucible – Intentional Suffering for Men</p><p>12:30 Plant Medicine, Breathwork &amp; Deep Healing</p><p>16:40 Faith, Surrender &amp; Letting Go of Control</p><p>21:30 The Crucible Experience – What It’s Really Like</p><p>26:00 May 1-3 Crucible + May 3 Mental Health Walk</p><p>30:00 Final Advice: Show Up, Don’t Quit, Smile (Your Face Is Your Fault)</p><p><strong>More About Luke:</strong></p><p>Check out Luke’s upcoming events at <u><a href="https://lukekayyem.com/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">LukeKayyem.com</a></u></p><p>Follow Luke on Instagram: @lukekayyem</p>
/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[27]/description[1]=<p>Entrepreneur, speaker, peak performance coach, and founder of <em>Fathers of the Future</em>, <strong>Luke Kayyem</strong> shares his raw, no-excuses story. Born to a single mom, haunted by an absent father who was in and out of prison, Luke spiraled into rage, multiple DUIs, bar fights, and self-destruction — until a near-miss with his newborn daughter became the wake-up call that changed everything.</p><p>Now 17+ years sober, Luke opens up about intentional suffering, plant medicine journeys, surrendering to faith, and building <em>The Crucible</em> — his legendary 32-hour men’s transformation experience that combines physical, mental, emotional, and spiritual challenges.</p><p>If you’re a man struggling with purpose, identity, trauma, or just ready for the next level — this episode hits hard.</p><p><strong>Key Topics:</strong></p><ul><li>Growing up fatherless and the rage that followed</li><li>Rock bottom: DUIs, fights, losing everything</li><li>The moment his daughter was born that forced change</li><li>Sobriety, CrossFit, and rebuilding identity</li><li>Plant medicine, breathwork &amp; intentional healing</li><li>The Crucible: 32-hour men’s transformation (what really happens)</li><li>Faith, surrender, and becoming the man you needed</li><li>Fathers of the Future movement &amp; helping the next generation</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters:</strong></p><p>00:00 Intro &amp; Luke Kayyem’s Powerful Story Begins</p><p>01:53 Fatherless Childhood &amp; Early Trauma</p><p>04:45 From Rage &amp; Alcohol to Sobriety Wake-Up Call</p><p>08:20 Building The Crucible – Intentional Suffering for Men</p><p>12:30 Plant Medicine, Breathwork &amp; Deep Healing</p><p>16:40 Faith, Surrender &amp; Letting Go of Control</p><p>21:30 The Crucible Experience – What It’s Really Like</p><p>26:00 May 1-3 Crucible + May 3 Mental Health Walk</p><p>30:00 Final Advice: Show Up, Don’t Quit, Smile (Your Face Is Your Fault)</p><p><strong>More About Luke:</strong></p><p>Check out Luke’s upcoming events at <u><a href="https://lukekayyem.com/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">LukeKayyem.com</a></u></p><p>Follow Luke on Instagram: @lukekayyem</p>

/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[29]/title[1]=Trolls, Trauma, and Trophies | Greatest Hits
/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[2]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Henry Kellem lived big enough for both of him and his little brother.</p><p>A 12-gauge shotgun at point blank range totaled the car. Not one piece of glass hit him or his friend. He still does not know why God spared him that day.</p><p>Born to a 17-year-old mom. Rain inside the trailer. Brother shot in the head at 13. Principal called him scum of the earth. Busted for weed at 18.</p><p>He told Mike Lindstrom and Scott Leese how he still graduated. Still got the degrees. Still moved to Beverly Hills with nothing but a dream from The Beverly Hillbillies.</p><p>Then he met his daughter. Everything changed. Abundance stopped meaning money. "I am" became the fight against the voice that said he was not good enough.</p><p>He is writing the book now. Dirt Road to Rodeo. Not the zip code. The journey.</p><p>If you have ever felt too small for the room you walked into, start here.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>How a little voice told him to front two extra pounds and kept him out of prison</li><li>Why changing your zip code does not change the voice in your head</li><li>What abundance looks like when your daughter grabs your hand and says school shoes, snacks, and Johnjay also explores the challenges of monetizing a brand and handling criticism in the public eye, all while maintaining authenticity and connection with his audience. In this engaging conversation, the speakers share their transformative journeys, highlighting the power of mentorship, personal growth, and overcoming adversity. They discuss their experiences with health innovations, including biohacking and natural healing, and reflect on influential literature that has shaped their lives. The conversation also touches on their passion for dog rescue through the Love Pup Foundation and their aspirations for the future. Memorable celebrity encounters and interviews provide a nostalgic backdrop to their stories, showcasing the impact of personal connections and the importance of setting goals.</p><p><b>Takeaways</b></p><p>+Johnjay&apos;s journey into radio began as an intern in 1990.<br/>+He emphasizes the importance of vulnerability in connecting with audiences.<br/>+Content creation should be spontaneous and authentic.<br/>+Investing in local talent can lead to viral success.<br/>+Family support is crucial in pursuing one&apos;s dreams.<br/>+Personal loss has shaped John Jay&apos;s perspective on life.<br/>+Comedy influences from childhood helped shape his career.<br/>+Monetizing a brand can be a complex decision.<br/>+Handling criticism requires a thick skin and perspective.<br/>+Building connections with guests enhances the podcast experience. /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[40]/content:encoded[1]=<p>In this episode, Mike and Scott sit down with <b>Jeremy Beer</b> for a wide-ranging conversation on philanthropy, entrepreneurship, and the deeper motivations that drive people to give, build, and belong.</p><p>Jeremy shares his <em>unexpected journey</em> from aspiring academic to co-founding a successful philanthropic consulting firm, offering a candid look at how unplanned paths often lead to the most meaningful work. </p><p>The conversation explores the <b>psychology of giving</b>, challenging the idea that philanthropy is driven purely by data and efficiency. Instead, Jeremy explains how identity, belonging, and emotional connection play a far greater role in motivating donors than spreadsheets ever could.</p><p>They also dive into the <b>real challenges of entrepreneurship</b>, including hiring well, sustaining company culture, and navigating the tension between mission and growth. Jeremy offers a thoughtful critique of the effective altruism movement, arguing that while logic matters, human emotion is often the true catalyst for generosity.</p><p>Jeremy opens up about his <b>writing process</b>, balancing collaborative business projects with deeply personal academic work, and addresses common <b>myths in the nonprofit sector</b>, including the overemphasis on low overhead ratios. </p><p>The episode wraps with reflections on community engagement, civil society, podcasting, and Jeremy’s upcoming book focused on a bold philanthropic bet—plus a fun look at his passion for traveling and visiting minor league baseball stadiums across the country.</p><p>Thanks for watching! If this episode moved you, hit LIKE, drop a comment with your biggest takeaway, and SUBSCRIBE for more raw stories, mindset shifts, and real conversations.</p><p>New episodes drop weekly!</p><p><b>TikTok:</b> <a href='https://tiktok.com/@wys_whatsyourstor'>tiktok.com/@wys_whatsyourstor</a><br/><b>Instagram:</b> <a href='https://instagram.com/whatsyourstorypod_com'>instagram.com/whatsyourstorypod_com</a><br/><b>X:</b> <a href='https://x.com/WYSPodcast_com'>x.com/WYSPodcast_com</a><br/><b>Facebook:</b> <a href='https://facebook.com/WhatsYourStoryPod'>facebook.com/WhatsYourStoryPod</a></p><p><b>Mike&apos;s Website:</b> <a href='https://mikelindstrom.com'>mikelindstrom.com</a><br/><b>Scott&apos;s Website:</b> <a href='https://scottleeseconsulting.com'>scottleeseconsulting.com</a></p><p>Show website coming soon!</p>

Tom reflects on his early sales experiences, the transition to entrepreneurship, and the challenges he faced along the way. He emphasizes the importance of finding passion and purpose in one&apos;s work, as well as the impact of parenting on his own life. Tom&apos;s story is a testament to the power of perseverance and the desire to help others succeed.</p><p><b>Takeaways</b></p><p>+Tom&apos;s journey is shaped by his childhood experiences and family influences.<br/>+Connection and empathy are key skills in sales and leadership.<br/>+Sales is not just about tactics; it&apos;s about being human.<br/>+Entrepreneurship requires resilience and adaptability.<br/>+Finding passion in work is essential for fulfillment.<br/>+Parenting plays a significant role in shaping one&apos;s values and perspectives.<br/>+Tom emphasizes the importance of community in personal and professional growth.<br/>+The transition from employee to entrepreneur can be challenging but rewarding.<br/>+Tom&apos;s approach to sales focuses on building relationships rather than just closing deals.<br/>+Success in sales comes from consistency and genuine effort. </p><p><b>Chapters</b></p><p>00:00 Introduction to Tom Slocum&apos;s Journey<br/>02:57 Childhood and Family Influences<br/>06:01 The Impact of Early Experiences on Personal Growth<br/>08:47 Sales Career Beginnings and Lessons Learned<br/>11:36 The Evolution of Sales Skills and Techniques<br/>14:44 Transitioning to Entrepreneurship<br/>17:44 Navigating Challenges in Entrepreneurship<br/>20:31 Building a Community and Supporting Others<br/>23:42 Parenting and Its Influence on Career Choices<br/>26:42 Future Aspirations and Community Engagement</p><p>Thanks for watching! /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[42]/content:encoded[1]=<p>In this episode, Carey Pena shares her journey from a successful career in journalism to becoming an entrepreneur and documentarian. She discusses the challenges of balancing motherhood with her career, the importance of authenticity in storytelling, and her passion for addressing social issues through her work. </p><p>Carey emphasizes the significance of relationships, the joy of storytelling, and the need for innovative approaches in the nonprofit sector. She reflects on her family influences, the lessons learned from her experiences, and her commitment to using her platform for good.</p><p><b>Takeaways</b></p><p>+Carey knew she wanted to be a journalist from age 16.<br/>+She transitioned from news to entrepreneurship to learn more about business.<br/>+Balancing motherhood and career is challenging but rewarding.<br/>+Entrepreneurship requires hard work and a willingness to learn.<br/>+Innovative storytelling can help nonprofits thrive, especially during crises.<br/>+Authenticity in storytelling is crucial for connecting with audiences.<br/>+Carey&apos;s documentary &apos;Blank Canvas&apos; explores rehabilitation in prisons.<br/>+Family influences shaped Carey&apos;s work ethic and values.<br/>+Finding purpose in heartbreak drives Carey&apos;s passion for social issues.<br/>+Carey advises not be intimidated by any room you&apos;re in.</p><p><b>Chapters</b></p><p>00:00 Introduction to Carey Pena<br/>02:00 Carey&apos;s Journey into Journalism<br/>06:07 Transitioning from News to Entrepreneurship<br/>12:21 Balancing Motherhood and Career<br/>15:09 The Challenges of Entrepreneurship<br/>20:20 Innovative Storytelling in Nonprofits<br/>24:12 Authenticity in Storytelling<br/>29:56 Exploring the Documentary &apos;Blank Canvas&apos;<br/>36:12 Lessons from Family and Influences<br/>39:55 Finding Purpose in Heartbreak<br/>45:18 Advice to My Younger Self<br/>51:57 What&apos;s Next for Carey Pena?</p><p>Thanks for watching! /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[43]/content:encoded[1]=<p>In this episode, Mike Lindstrom and Scott Leese welcome Patrick Antrim, founder and CEO of Nectar Flow, to discuss his journey from professional athlete to entrepreneur and the transformative role of AI in business. Patrick shares insights on the importance of continuous learning, the challenges of transitioning from sports to business, and how AI can enhance productivity and drive growth. He emphasizes the importance of effective leadership in navigating the complexities of AI implementation and offers practical advice for businesses seeking to leverage technology for success.</p><p><b>Takeaways</b></p><p>+Patrick Antrim emphasizes the importance of continuous learning in both sports and business.<br/>+The transition from professional athlete to entrepreneur can provide unique insights into leadership.<br/>+AI is not just a tool; it&apos;s a transformative force in business.<br/>+Understanding bottlenecks in business is crucial for growth and efficiency.<br/>+Effective leadership is essential for successful AI adoption.<br/>+The future of work will require adaptability and a willingness to embrace change.<br/>+AI can enhance productivity by automating repetitive tasks and providing insights.<br/>+Creating a culture of learning and innovation is vital for organizations.<br/>+The five manageable tasks for successful AI projects can significantly increase success rates.<br/>+Trust and credibility are key components in the adoption of new technologies. </p><p><b>Chapters</b></p><p>00:00 Introduction to Patrick Antrim and AI Innovations<br/>02:50 Patrick&apos;s Journey from Professional Athlete to Entrepreneur<br/>05:55 The Importance of Learning and Adaptation in Business<br/>08:47 Navigating the Transition from Baseball to Business<br/>11:45 Identifying Bottlenecks in Business and Leveraging AI<br/>14:41 Preparing for the Future: AI and Job Security<br/>17:38 The Role of AI in Enhancing Business Processes<br/>20:41 Real-Time AI Applications and Their Impact on Hiring<br/>23:55 Concerns About AI Misuse<br/>27:27 Navigating AI in Business<br/>30:05 Five Manageable Tasks for AI Success<br/>33:59 Leadership Insights and First Principles<br/>38:53 Lessons from Sports and Personal Growth<br/>42:25 Innovations in AI and Technology Center</p><p>Thanks for watching! Buying followers might take the top spot, but it’s a surprisingly close race.</p><p>In this anything-goes jam session, Mike Lindstrom &amp; Scott Leese hold nothing back. No shared notes. Mike rates the corporate buzzwords we all love to hate. Is “synergy” the worst? Does anyone actually have the “bandwidth”?</p><p>Then they rank the most common ways people fake it and which platforms will matter most for B2B in 2026. The conversation shifts to the WNBA’s brand, its growth, and the media lens on its players. Finally, they ask whether governments should make social media illegal for teens.</p><p>Get an inside look into Mike &amp; Scott's friendship as they grill each other and see if they can stay out of trouble.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>Mike’s letter-grade ranking of corporate buzzwords</li><li>The ranking of fake business moves, from staged photos to buying followers</li><li>A blind ranking of B2B platforms for 2026</li><li>The WNBA’s brand and the media lens on its players</li><li>The debate over government regulation of social media for teenagers</li><li>Ideal no-obligation Saturdays and what they reveal</li><li>Travel styles from road trips to cruises</li><li>Ways people show off wealth</li><li>Why some phrases are permanently off-limits</li><li>The mid-game social media rule most pro sports already enforce</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 Cold Open: The WNBA Discussion</li><li>01:46 Welcome to the Jam Session</li><li>02:53 Mike Ranks Corporate Buzzwords</li><li>04:57 Blind Ranking: B2B Thought Leadership Platforms in 2026</li><li>07:28 Ranking Fake Business Moves</li><li>10:13 Blindsided: Scott Ranks His Ideal Saturday</li><li>12:18 Ranking Travel Styles</li><li>16:48 The Ultimate Wealth Flex Ranking</li><li>20:00 Hot Topic: The Full WNBA Discussion</li><li>24:34 Hot Topic: Should We Ban Social Media for Teens</li><li>28:17 Thanks For Jamming With Us</li></ul><br/><p>What’s YOUR most-hated corporate buzzword? /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[50]/title[1]=Arizona's "Mr. TV" on Authenticity, Energy & Life After Broadcasting
/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[51]/content:encoded[1]=<p>In this episode, Nick Capozzi shares his unique journey from selling art and jewelry on cruise ships to becoming a prominent figure in tech sales and biohacking. He discusses the importance of storytelling in sales, the impact of AI on the industry, and practical health tips for longevity. Nick emphasizes the significance of personalized medicine and the future of health, particularly in relation to peptides and biohacking. He provides a much-needed glimpse into the future of "longevity" and medicine.</p><p><strong>Takeaways</strong></p><p>+When everyone is homogenized, standing out is crucial.</p><p>+Differentiation can be a key to success in branding.</p><p>+Using metaphors can help convey complex ideas simply.</p><p>+Creativity is essential in a competitive market.</p><p>+Being different can attract attention and interest.</p><p>+Brands should strive to be unique rather than conform to the status quo.</p><p>+The example of Norwegian cruise lines illustrates effective branding.</p><p>+Finding your unique angle can lead to more effective marketing strategies.</p><p>+Embracing individuality can foster innovation.</p><p>+In a crowded market, uniqueness can be your greatest asset.</p><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><p>00:00 Introduction to Longevity and Biohacking</p><p>03:00 Nick's Journey: From Cruise Ships to Tech Sales</p><p>06:04 The Art of Selling on Cruise Ships</p><p>08:47 Transitioning to Tech Sales and Coaching</p><p>12:08 The Impact of AI on Sales and Marketing</p><p>19:04 The Evolution of AI Tools in Video Production</p><p>20:53 Authenticity in Personal Branding</p><p>23:19 Exploring Healthspan and Biohacking</p><p>27:24 Navigating the Health and Longevity Space</p><p>31:11 Practical Tips for Health Improvement</p><p>36:06 Future Directions in Health and Functional Medicine</p><p><strong>Sound Bites</strong></p><p>"How can I be a little bit different than everyone else? If everyone else is vanilla, how can I be chocolate?"</p><p>"When everyone is homogenized and everyone is going in the same direction, how can you be a fish going in the other direction?"</p><p>"I think the biggest learning for me is that you've heard the 10,000-hour rule. /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[54]/content:encoded[1]=<p>In this episode of the What's Your Story podcast, Scott Leese and Mike Lindstrom sit down with Morgan J. /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[56]/title[1]=NHL Star Dave Scatchard on Concussions, Rock Bottom, and True Authenticity
/rss[1]/channel[1]/item[5]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Ian Lopatin did not post anything for 53 years. He built Spiritual Gangster, sold a yoga studio chain to Lifetime Fitness, and stayed behind the brand.</p><p>He is back on What's Your Story with Mike Lindstrom and Scott Leese, almost a year to the day after episode 5. Two years sober next week. His book, The Way of a Spiritual Gangster, comes out 10-10. The shirt says Stress Sober... but it doesn't mean what you think.</p><p>There are only two states, he says. Stress, where 99 percent of people live. And stress sober, where the miracles are. You do not manage the drinking. You abstain. Same move with stress. No amount of success gets you out. He knows wealthy friends who are some of the most stressed people in the room.</p><p>Then he gets to the line his mom left him: "Illness starts with I. Wellness starts with we." Eight years since she died, and he is still hearing it. Surrender, in his new definition, is switching to the winning team. Not giving up. Getting to a higher frequency.</p><p>The loop the cold open opens does not close until the middle. Fear. The studios. The Lifetime sale. Spiritual Gangster out front, Ian behind it. The book forced him to be the voice. For 53 years he did not post anything. The last couple of months he has been learning in public.</p><p>Later he talks about the friends who fell away, the wife who said she did not know who he was, and the billionaires still trying to fill the last jar. If you accumulate all of it and die with it, what are you doing?</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>Selling a yoga studio chain to Lifetime Fitness</li><li>The opposite of addiction is connection</li><li>Six weeks in India and obsessive self-centric thinking</li><li>Off course to of course, and removing one F</li><li>Why his YPO forum stopped saying unbelievable</li><li>His mom's yahrzeit and the eight-year anniversary</li><li>Joe Dispenza, personality, and personal reality</li><li>The caterpillar, the goo, and the butterfly on the book cover</li><li>The pineal gland, the temples, and the cell phone analogy</li><li>Teaching the outline in law school to finish top of the class</li><li>32 shows at The Sphere, and an enlightenment idea for it</li><li>Worry as the worst use of your imagination</li><li>A 23-minute Zoom meditation he has not missed in two years</li><li>Alcohol as poison. Every day. Not a fall-down drunk.</li><li>Billionaires living in lack, and the seven jars of gold</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 Afraid to Be the Face</li><li>00:40 What's Your Story</li><li>01:35 A Year Later, Same Studio</li><li>03:07 The Way of a Spiritual Gangster</li><li>03:33 Stress Sober</li><li>04:52 Two Years Sober Next Week</li><li>06:50 Of Course Is a Frequency</li><li>8:30 Live Events and the Egypt Eclipse</li><li>09:58 A New Definition of Surrender</li><li>11:08 Illness Starts With I</li><li>13:12 53 Years Without a Post</li><li>14:27 Grow or Graduate</li><li>15:56 Vanessa Said I Don't Know Who You Are</li><li>18:43 The Install, Not the Realization</li><li>19:51 Joe Dispenza and the Field</li><li>21:14 What an Of Course Weekend Is</li><li>23:50 A Million Books and The Sphere</li><li>25:51 Do What You Love</li><li>26:23 Ian Does Not Have Bad Days</li><li>27:40 Three Fingers Pointing Back</li><li>31:44 Meditate, Cold Plunge, Breath</li><li>33:02 Watches, Cars, and Intention</li><li>35:35 Protecting the Brand, Not Building It</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Connect with Ian</strong></p><ul><li><strong>Website:</strong> https://ianlopatin.com</li><li><strong>Book:</strong> The Way of a Spiritual Gangster, https://ianlopatin.com/book</li><li><strong>Instagram:</strong> https://www.instagram.com/iamianlopatin</li></ul><br/><p>Episode 5 is the first sit-down. /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[6]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Mike Lindstrom and Scott Leese started What's Your Story on the side of the road after a blown tire.</p><p>51 episodes later they unpack the podcast mistakes that almost killed Year One.</p><p>No guest. No script.</p><p>Just the two of them on the couch talking about a lunch with Dan Lier, a dead spare, and the moment Mike said "maybe they should just start a podcast."</p><p>They did. Dave Skatchard sat down first and talked about concussions and addiction. Ian Lopatin talked about surrender.</p><p>The downloads came later. So did the sponsors, the YouTube benchmarks, and the days they forgot to order lunch.</p><p>This is the season finale. Year One in the rearview. Year Two still unwritten.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>Why they refused to launch from FOMO like everybody else</li><li>Combining an insurance brand with a go-to-market brand</li><li>The YouTube subscriber and watch-time marks that finally moved</li><li>Listening to guests as teachers instead of content</li><li>God winks from Dave Skatchard and Ian Lopatin</li><li>Applying guest advice to health, kids, and coaching</li><li>Why fake downloads feel good and still leave you empty</li><li>Booking two and a half months out instead of three weeks out</li><li>Taking the show to Austin, Kansas City, St. Louis, and Miami</li><li>Coaching other founders who want to copy the format</li><li>Alice Cooper, Charles Barkley, Kurt Warner, and Jerry Colangelo</li><li>Co-parenting, health, and catching up after every shoot</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 Dave Skatchard sat down and got real</li><li>00:41 Show intro</li><li>01:35 One year on the couch</li><li>01:55 The blown tire that started the show</li><li>02:55 First taping week in Scottsdale</li><li>04:06 North of a million impressions</li><li>05:16 Robert, YouTube, and the first sponsors</li><li>06:02 How the guests changed Mike</li><li>08:42 How year one changed Scott</li><li>09:59 Dan Lier should not even be here</li><li>11:16 Getting invested in the guest journeys</li><li>11:45 Three mistakes that could have killed us</li><li>12:31 Fake views and the dopamine hit</li><li>13:50 We did not know which platform to pick</li><li>14:22 The line between authentic and unprepared</li><li>16:12 Susan McGalla and Jon McQueen</li><li>17:29 Year two on the road</li><li>19:46 Highway 99 Media</li><li>21:14 Dream Phoenix guests</li><li>23:20 The real cost of flying 2,000 miles</li><li>25:00 A 10-hour shoot day</li><li>26:33 Nodding off on Scottsdale Road</li><li>27:31 Thank you and Year Two</li></ul><br/><p>If you have been here since episode one, /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[7]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Rich Berra says he will probably die behind the microphone. After 25 years of JohnJay and Rich, he knows he was born for it. There is still no Plan B.</p><p>He told Mike Lindstrom &amp; Scott Leese the whole climb. A five-year-old in a leaky 76 Pinto hears Foreigner's "Hot Blooded" and Don McLean's "American Pie." He begs for a guitar. The Italian family in St. Louis can only swing accordion rentals. Then a grandfather buys the guitar, a 14-year-old rides his bike to a 100-watt YMCA station, and at 16 he is doing weekend overnights in downtown St. Louis, cleaning grocery stores at 4 a.m., sitting with his mom in hospice, and going to high school.</p><p>That kid is now half of one of the longest-running morning shows in the country. ⁨JohnJay &amp; Rich. iHeartRadio. KISS FM. Tucson to Phoenix and markets all over North America. Rich still wakes up at 3:25. He still preps like he is auditioning to keep the job tomorrow.</p><p>He still says they have never had a great show.</p><p>He also talks about the parts that do not fit in a liner. War of the Roses and the guy who found him at the mall with his kids. A lifetime ban from Davis-Monthan Air Force Base. Phones that were obviously tapped. A Secret Service email that said stop bragging. Five kids, a daughter leaving for New York, and a charity arm called Love Up that is trying to get 14,000 Arizona foster kids closer to a permanent home.</p><p>If you grew up with a radio in the dash, or you still wonder whether that medium has a future, start here.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>How a carpool ride and "Hot Blooded" put a five-year-old on a radio path</li><li>Accordion rentals, a first guitar, and a 100-watt YMCA station in 1984</li><li>Working overnights at 16 while cleaning grocery stores and sitting hospice</li><li>Why 90-plus percent of Americans still listen every week</li><li>Cameras in the studio, live streaming, and adapting or leaving the business</li><li>How JohnJay and Rich split social media without splitting the brand</li><li>War of the Roses, a mall confrontation, and bits that follow you home</li><li>Secret Service interest, tapped phones, and a ban from Davis-Monthan</li><li>Ratings versus clients versus him</li><li>Meeting JohnJay in a New Orleans lobby and choosing Tucson over Cincinnati</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 Cold Open</li><li>00:38 Show intro</li><li>01:33 /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[8]/content:encoded[1]=<p>John Moriarty says the number one money mistake is treating net worth like a scoreboard. Regular earners do it. The 1% does it too.</p><p>He is back on What’s Your Story? with Mike &amp; Scott after his first appearance in Episode 32. This time the conversation is the business, the kids, and the beliefs that quietly run the bank account. John built e3 Wealth and co-wrote The Entrepreneur Playbook. Thirty years in finance and he still starts with the same question. What is your relationship with money?</p><p>Scott asks if AI replaces the advisor. John is not worried. He is excited. Templates get cheaper. The relationship gets more valuable. If you do not understand how a client thinks about money, the software will not save you.</p><p>Then the parenting fight nobody wants. Scott tells his kids what he earns. Mike just wants to know why they are asking.</p><p>John splits the difference. Make it an adult conversation. Ask what they will do with the answer before you hand them the number. Teach the expense column, not just the paycheck. Dreams are not free. Snowplow the first two steps if you have to. Do not carry the torch.</p><p>He ranks the flexes too. And he will clap for the person who owns #1.</p><p>If you have been waiting to talk money with your teenagers, or you keep checking the scoreboard and calling it a plan, start here.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>Why software makes the human part of money advice more important, not less</li><li>What to do when a kid asks what the family is worth</li><li>How scarcity sneaks back in after someone finally gets rich</li><li>Giving teenagers a real P&amp;L instead of another pep talk</li><li>Helping the next generation without walking the path for them</li><li>The difference between conviction and Monday morning quarterbacking</li><li>Quiet wealth versus the stuff people buy to prove they arrived</li><li>Using the tax code as a set of incentives instead of a wall</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 Cold Open: Ranking Wealth Flexes</li><li>00:40 Show Intro</li><li>01:35 FOMAS and Why John Is Back</li><li>03:15 What Changed Since Episode 32</li><li>04:24 Elevator Pitch of John's Life</li><li>05:29 Will AI Replace Financial Advisors?</li><li>08:10 Personalization Beats Templates</li><li>08:46 Should You Tell Your Kids Your Net Worth?</li><li>09:55 Abundance vs Scarcity by Generation</li><li>13:41 The Number One Money Mistake</li><li>16:07 The 1% Scarcity Trap</li><li>18:19 Working With Entrepreneurs</li><li>19:15 3 Must-Have Money Talks With Teens</li><li>20:26 Teach Bills, Not Just Paychecks</li><li>22:04 Dreams Have a Price</li><li>24:39 Snowplow Parenting and Embrace the Suck</li><li>26:29 Open the Door. Make Them Walk Through It</li><li>28:29 Do Not Teach Money as Status</li><li>29:17 Loss Aversion in Real Life</li><li>31:14 The 10-Year Flat Amazon Chart</li><li>33:14 Private Jets Beat Watches and Cars</li><li>35:15 Building e3 Wealth Across 50 States</li><li>36:19 The Entrepreneur Playbook</li><li>37:12 Read the Tax Code as Incentives</li><li>39:12 Educate, Empower, Enlighten</li><li>40:30 Wrap and How to Follow John</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Episode Links</strong></p><ul><li>Website: https://e3wealth.com</li><li>The Entrepreneur Playbook: https://www.amazon.com/Entrepreneur-Playbook-Color-Cracking-Strategies/dp/B0FVLTL4H3</li><li>First episode with John: https://youtu.be/DlxgPQ4Ykb4</li></ul><br/><p>Did your parents treat money like a scoreboard, or did they treat it like a tool? Thirty years in finance and he still starts with the same question. What is your relationship with money?</p><p>Scott asks if AI replaces the advisor. John is not worried. He is excited. Templates get cheaper. The relationship gets more valuable. If you do not understand how a client thinks about money, the software will not save you.</p><p>Then the parenting fight nobody wants. Scott tells his kids what he earns. Mike just wants to know why they are asking.</p><p>John splits the difference. Make it an adult conversation. Ask what they will do with the answer before you hand them the number. Teach the expense column, not just the paycheck. Dreams are not free. Snowplow the first two steps if you have to. Do not carry the torch.</p><p>He ranks the flexes too. And he will clap for the person who owns #1.</p><p>If you have been waiting to talk money with your teenagers, or you keep checking the scoreboard and calling it a plan, start here.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>Why software makes the human part of money advice more important, not less</li><li>What to do when a kid asks what the family is worth</li><li>How scarcity sneaks back in after someone finally gets rich</li><li>Giving teenagers a real P&amp;L instead of another pep talk</li><li>Helping the next generation without walking the path for them</li><li>The difference between conviction and Monday morning quarterbacking</li><li>Quiet wealth versus the stuff people buy to prove they arrived</li><li>Using the tax code as a set of incentives instead of a wall</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Chapters</strong></p><ul><li>00:00 Cold Open: Ranking Wealth Flexes</li><li>00:40 Show Intro</li><li>01:35 FOMAS and Why John Is Back</li><li>03:15 What Changed Since Episode 32</li><li>04:24 Elevator Pitch of John's Life</li><li>05:29 Will AI Replace Financial Advisors?</li><li>08:10 Personalization Beats Templates</li><li>08:46 Should You Tell Your Kids Your Net Worth?</li><li>09:55 Abundance vs Scarcity by Generation</li><li>13:41 The Number One Money Mistake</li><li>16:07 The 1% Scarcity Trap</li><li>18:19 Working With Entrepreneurs</li><li>19:15 3 Must-Have Money Talks With Teens</li><li>20:26 Teach Bills, Not Just Paychecks</li><li>22:04 Dreams Have a Price</li><li>24:39 Snowplow Parenting and Embrace the Suck</li><li>26:29 Open the Door. Make Them Walk Through It</li><li>28:29 Do Not Teach Money as Status</li><li>29:17 Loss Aversion in Real Life</li><li>31:14 The 10-Year Flat Amazon Chart</li><li>33:14 Private Jets Beat Watches and Cars</li><li>35:15 Building e3 Wealth Across 50 States</li><li>36:19 The Entrepreneur Playbook</li><li>37:12 Read the Tax Code as Incentives</li><li>39:12 Educate, Empower, Enlighten</li><li>40:30 Wrap and How to Follow John</li></ul><br/><p><strong>Episode Links</strong></p><ul><li>Website: https://e3wealth.com</li><li>The Entrepreneur Playbook: https://www.amazon.com/Entrepreneur-Playbook-Color-Cracking-Strategies/dp/B0FVLTL4H3</li><li>First episode with John: https://youtu.be/DlxgPQ4Ykb4</li></ul><br/><p>Did your parents treat money like a scoreboard, or did they treat it like a tool? /rss[1]/channel[1]/item[9]/content:encoded[1]=<p>Gabe Cooper told his own son not to take a software developer job. Then he made 180 people at Virtuous build AI agents from scratch so they would not get replaced by somebody who already knows AI.</p><p>He told Mike Lindstrom and Scott Leese the line his team did not want to hear. You are not going to get replaced by AI necessarily. You are definitely going to get replaced by somebody who knows AI. If you leave because of that, you will walk into the same problem somewhere that is not as generous.</p><p>That is the tension inside this episode of What's Your Story? with Mike and Scott. Gabe is the Phoenix founder and CEO of Virtuous, the nonprofit CRM people call HubSpot for charities. Fifth straight year on the Inc. 5000. About $100 million Series C. A company mission to create $10 billion in net new generosity. Almost $2 billion of that is already live through customers.</p><p>The path there is messy on purpose. West Texas. A record label that made zero dollars. Golf Shot with Tiger Woods and Paul Azinger. Selling a consumer app company because it felt like eight seconds on a bull. Then 12 years of Virtuous, a mortgage, five kids, and the kind of endurance he now compares to a pain cave at mile 20.</p><p>He also says this is the last company. COVID. Every operating dollar in Silicon Valley Bank on the day it collapsed, found out on a pickleball court with a beer in his hand. Now AI. He is done collecting black swans.</p><p><strong>Topics Covered</strong></p><ul><li>West Texas, a high school sweetheart, five kids, and the rockstar plan that did not pay</li><li>How you know when to
